<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every type II<sub>1</sub> factor with separable predual is generated by one operator, equivalently by two self-adjoint operators. Combined with the established direct-integral reduction to this case, this gives an affirmative solution of the generator problem for von Neumann algebras with separable predual.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-relative-generation)): Let $P\subset M$ be an irreducible inclusion of type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors, with $M$ of separable predual. The set $$\{u\in\mathcal U(M):W^*(P,u)=M\}$$ is a dense $G_\delta$ subset of the unitary group $\mathcal U(M)$ for the trace $2$-norm topology.
  - Explanation: For any irreducible inclusion of type II_1 factors with separable predual, the unitaries that generate the ambient factor relative to the subfactor form a dense G_delta subset in the trace 2-norm topology.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M$ with separable predual is generated by two self-adjoint elements. Equivalently, there is an $x\in M$ such that $M=W^*(x)$.
  - Explanation: Every separable-predual type II_1 factor is generated as a von Neumann algebra by two self-adjoint elements, or equivalently by a single operator.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ultrapower-commutant)): The inclusion $\mathbf Q\subset\mathbf M$ is irreducible: $$\mathbf Q'\cap\mathbf M=\mathbb C1.$$
  - Explanation: Irreducibility of a subfactor inclusion passes to the tracial ultrapower inclusion.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-popa-independence)): Let $P\subset M$ be an irreducible inclusion of $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors, and put $\mathbf Q=P^\omega\subset M^\omega=\mathbf M$. For every linear subspace $X\subset\mathbf M$ that is separable in $2$-norm and satisfies $\tau(x)=0$ for all $x\in X$, there is a diffuse abelian von Neumann algebra $A\subset\mathbf Q$ such that $$\label{eq:popa-independence} \tau(x_0a_1x_1\cdots a_mx_m)=0$$ whenever $m\geq1$, $x_0\in X\cup\{1\}$, $x_1,\ldots,x_m\in X$, and $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in A$ have trace zero.
  - Explanation: Popa independence supplies a diffuse abelian subalgebra inside the subfactor ultrapower that is freely independent from any prescribed 2-norm separable centered subspace.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-free-haar-extension)): Let $D\subset\mathbf M$ be a norm-separable unital $C^*$-algebra. There is a Haar unitary $t\in\mathbf Q$ free from $D$. In particular, $$\label{eq:free-endpoints} \tau(d_0t^{n_1}d_1\cdots t^{n_j}d_j)=0$$ for every $j\geq1$, every $n_1,\ldots,n_j\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$, and all $d_0,\ldots,d_j\in D$ such that $\tau(d_i)=0$ for $1\leq i\leq j-1$. The two endpoints $d_0,d_j$ are arbitrary.
  - Explanation: Every norm-separable unital C*-subalgebra in the ultrapower admits a free Haar unitary inside the subfactor ultrapower, with vanishing trace on alternating words with arbitrary endpoints.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-haar-absorption)): Let $D$ be a unital $C^*$-subalgebra of a finite tracial von Neumann algebra, and let $t$ be a Haar unitary free from $D$. If $a,b\in\mathcal U(D)$, then both $atb$ and $t^*$ are Haar unitaries free from $D$. Both satisfy <a href="#eq:free-endpoints" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:free-endpoints">[eq:free-endpoints]</a> in place of $t$.
  - Explanation: Multiplying a free Haar unitary on either side by unitaries from the base C*-subalgebra, or taking its adjoint, preserves the Haar property and freeness.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-length-one-convolution)): Let $F$ be a free group with a specified free basis, let $\mathcal L$ be the set of basis elements and their inverses, and let $\lambda$ be the left regular representation on $\ell^2(F)$. For every finitely supported family $(b_\gamma)_{\gamma\in\mathcal L}$, $$\lVert \sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal L}b_\gamma\lambda(\gamma)\rVert \le 2\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal L}\|b_\gamma\|^2\right)^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The operator norm of a length-one convolution operator on a free group is bounded by twice the l2 norm of its coefficients.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-perturbation)): Let $P\subset M$ be an irreducible inclusion of $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors. Given $u,z\in\mathcal U(M)$, $\xi\in M$, and $0<\alpha<1/2$, for every integer $\ell\ge1$ there are $\widetilde u\in\mathcal U(M)$ and $\widetilde t_0,\ldots,\widetilde t_\ell\in\mathcal U(P)$ such that, with $$\widetilde W=\widetilde t_0 \prod_{k=1}^{\ell}(\widetilde u\,\widetilde t_k),$$ one has $$\label{eq:perturbation-bounds} \begin{aligned} \lVert \widetilde u-u\rVert_2 &\le \frac{\alpha}{\sqrt{2\ell}}+\frac1\ell,\\ \left\|\tau(\xi^*\widetilde W) -\frac\alpha2\tau(\xi^*z)\right\| &\le \lVert \xi\rVert_2\frac{4\alpha^2}{1-2\alpha}+\frac1\ell. \end{aligned}$$ All products indexed by $k$ are taken in increasing order from left to right. The unitaries may depend on $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Provides a small trace 2-norm perturbation of a unitary together with subfactor unitaries whose alternating word of length ell achieves a prescribed first-order correlation with a uniform quadratic remainder bound.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-unitary-completeness)): The Hilbert space $L^2(M,\tau)$ is separable, and $\mathcal U(M)$ is complete for the metric $d(u,v)=\lVert u-v\rVert_2$.
  - Explanation: For a separable-predual finite factor, the trace Hilbert space is separable and the unitary group is a complete metric space under the trace 2-norm distance.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projection-continuity)): Each $f_\xi$ is bounded and lower semicontinuous on $(\mathcal U(M),d)$. There is a dense $G_\delta$ subset $\mathcal C\subset\mathcal U(M)$ such that every $f_\xi$, $\xi\in L^2(M,\tau)$, is continuous at every point of $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: The L2 projection norm onto the relative generated subalgebra is lower semicontinuous in the unitary variable, yielding a dense G_delta set of simultaneous continuity points across all vectors.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-irreducible-hyperfinite)): Let $N\subset M$ be an irreducible inclusion of $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable preduals. There is a hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ subfactor $R\subset N$ such that $R'\cap M=\mathbb C1$.
  - Explanation: Popa irreducible hyperfinite embedding theorem provides a hyperfinite subfactor inside any separable-predual type II_1 factor with trivial relative commutant.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-hyperfinite-generators)): The hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor with separable predual is generated by two self-adjoint elements.
  - Explanation: Constructs two self-adjoint generators for the separable-predual hyperfinite type II_1 factor by encoding two commuting families of matrix projections via ternary series.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-four-generators)): Every $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor with separable predual is generated by four self-adjoint elements.
  - Explanation: Combines two self-adjoint generators of an irreducible hyperfinite subfactor with the real and imaginary parts of a relative generating unitary to generate any separable-predual type II_1 factor with four self-adjoint elements.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-matrix-encoding)): If a unital von Neumann algebra $N$ is generated by four self-adjoint elements, then $M_2(N)$ is generated by two self-adjoint elements.
  - Explanation: Encodes four self-adjoint generators of a unital von Neumann algebra into two self-adjoint two-by-two matrices using spectral separation on the diagonal and identity off-diagonal entries.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-entropy-generators)): For every integer $n\geq3$, the factor $L(\mathbb F_n)$ with its canonical trace has two finite self-adjoint generating tuples on which both $\delta$ and $\delta_0$ take different values. In particular, neither dimension is determined by the generated tracial von Neumann algebra.
  - Explanation: Shows that Voiculescu microstates free entropy dimensions delta and delta_0 are not W*-algebra invariants by contrasting the canonical semicircular n-tuple with a two-element self-adjoint generating pair.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **generated von Neumann subalgebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a subset $S$ of a von Neumann algebra $M$, write $W^*(S)$ for the smallest unital von Neumann subalgebra containing $S$.
- **trace 2-norm** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite factor with normalized trace $\tau$, put $\lVert x\rVert_2=\tau(x^*x)^{1/2}$.
- **irreducible inclusion** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An inclusion $P\subset M$ is *irreducible* when $P'\cap M=\mathbb C1$.
- **unitary group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\mathcal U(M)$ for the unitary group of $M$.
- **separable finite von Neumann algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): This ensures separability of $L^2(M,\tau)$; it does not require operator-norm separability of $M$.
- **operator norm and L2 inner product** ([source](#pm-h-5)): We write $\lVert x\rVert$ for the operator norm and use the inner product $\langle a,b\rangle=\tau(b^*a)$ on $L^2(M,\tau)$, linear in the first variable.
- **conditional expectation and orthogonal projection** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For a von Neumann subalgebra $N\subset M$, $E_N$ denotes the trace-preserving conditional expectation and $e_N$ the orthogonal projection from $L^2(M,\tau)$ onto $L^2(N,\tau)$.
- **tracial ultrapower** ([source](#sec:independence)): We use the tracial ultrapower $$\mathbf M=M^\omega =\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,M)/\mathcal I_\omega, \qquad \mathcal I_\omega =\left\{(b_n)\in\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,M): \lim_{n\to\omega}\\|b_n\\|_2=0\right\},$$ and its von Neumann subalgebra $\mathbf Q=P^\omega$.
- **Haar unitary** ([source](#sec:independence)): A unitary $t$ in a tracial algebra is called *Haar* if $\tau(t^n)=0$ for every nonzero integer $n$.
- **free subalgebras** ([source](#sec:independence)): Two unital subalgebras are *free* if every alternating product of centered elements from the two subalgebras has trace zero.
- **projection norm function** ([source](#sec:relative-generation)): For $\xi\in L^2(M,\tau)$, put $f_\xi(u)=\lVert e_u\xi\rVert_2$.
- **generator invariants G and G_sa** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Let $G$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}$ be the generator invariants defined in (Dykema et al. 2008, Definition 2.7) [[DSSW2008](#ref-DSSW2008)]. They are nonnegative infima over finite generating sets and finite projection partitions; $G_{\mathrm{sa}}$ restricts the generating sets to self-adjoint elements.
- **microstates free entropy dimensions** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a bounded finite self-adjoint tuple $Z$ in a tracial von Neumann algebra, let $\delta(Z)$ and $\delta_0(Z)$ denote Voiculescu’s microstates free entropy dimension and its modified version, respectively (Voiculescu 2002, sec. 2.4) [[Voiculescu2002Entropy](#ref-Voiculescu2002Entropy)].

          "exact_quote": "We use the tracial ultrapower $$\\mathbf M=M^\\omega\n  =\\ell^\\infty(\\mathbb N,M)/\\mathcal I_\\omega,\n  \\qquad\n  \\mathcal I_\\omega\n  =\\left\\{(b_n)\\in\\ell^\\infty(\\mathbb N,M):\n       \\lim_{n\\to\\omega}\\|b_n\\|_2=0\\right\\},$$ and its von Neumann subalgebra $\\mathbf Q=P^\\omega$.",

      "exact_definition": "We use the tracial ultrapower $$\\mathbf M=M^\\omega\n  =\\ell^\\infty(\\mathbb N,M)/\\mathcal I_\\omega,\n  \\qquad\n  \\mathcal I_\\omega\n  =\\left\\{(b_n)\\in\\ell^\\infty(\\mathbb N,M):\n       \\lim_{n\\to\\omega}\\|b_n\\|_2=0\\right\\},$$ and its von Neumann subalgebra $\\mathbf Q=P^\\omega$.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-ultrapower-commutant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ultrapower-commutant): Convex hull norm minimization in L2 and ultralimit of trace values -> Trivial relative commutant of the subfactor ultrapower inside the ambient ultrapower
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-popa-independence) / [proof](#sec:independence): Specialization of Popa free independence theorem using triviality of the relative commutant -> Diffuse abelian subalgebra in the subfactor ultrapower with vanishing alternating traces
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-free-haar-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-free-haar-extension): Dyadic projection refinement in a diffuse abelian subalgebra and endpoint decomposition -> Haar unitary in the subfactor ultrapower free from the C*-subalgebra with arbitrary endpoints
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-haar-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-haar-absorption): Expansion of alternating words into sign blocks separated by centered conjugated elements -> Invariance of freeness and Haar property under left and right multiplication by base unitaries
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-length-one-convolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-length-one-convolution): Orthogonal decomposition of reduced words by initial letter and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality -> Length-one Haagerup operator norm bound
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-perturbation): Coordinated prefix-conjugated exponential perturbation in the ultrapower majorized by free-group convolution and lifted to a sequence coordinate -> Perturbed unitary and subfactor unitaries achieving target first-order correlation with uniform remainder
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-unitary-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-unitary-completeness): Weak-star metrizability of the unit ball and strong operator convergence of Cauchy multiplication operators -> Separability of L2(M, tau) and completeness of U(M) under the trace 2-norm metric
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-projection-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projection-continuity): Infimum distance over *-polynomials, Baire category theorem on U(M), and L2 contractivity -> Dense G_delta subset of simultaneous continuity points for all projection norm functions
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-relative-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projection-continuity-2): Contradiction at a simultaneous continuity point by pairing an orthogonal defect vector with the uniform perturbation word -> Dense G_delta relative generation of M over P by a single unitary
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-hyperfinite-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hyperfinite-generators): Ternary series spectral encoding of two commuting families of site projections in the infinite tensor product model -> Two self-adjoint generators for the separable-predual hyperfinite type II_1 factor
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-four-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-four-generators): Combine Popa irreducible hyperfinite subfactor embedding with two hyperfinite generators and one relative unitary generator -> Four self-adjoint generators for any separable-predual type II_1 factor
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-matrix-encoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-encoding): Two-by-two matrix encoding with spectrally separated diagonal blocks and off-diagonal identity cuts -> Two self-adjoint generators for the two-by-two matrix amplification M_2(N)
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-matrix-encoding-2): Pass to a half-trace corner factor, generate it by four self-adjoint elements, and apply the two-by-two matrix encoding -> Two self-adjoint generators (equivalently one operator generator) for M
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-cor-entropy-generators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-entropy-generators): Compare the microstates free entropy dimensions of the semicircular n-tuple with the two-element generating tuple from Theorem 2 -> Non-invariance of microstates free entropy dimensions delta and delta_0 on L(F_n) for n at least 3

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: For any irreducible inclusion $P\subset M$ of type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors where $M$ has separable predual, the relative generating locus of unitaries $u\in\mathcal U(M)$ satisfying $M=W^*(P,u)$ is a dense $G_\delta$ subset of $\mathcal U(M)$ in the trace $2$-norm metric $d(u,v)=\lVert u-v\rVert_2$.
- **I2**: Every type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M$ with separable predual is generated by two self-adjoint elements, equivalently by one operator $x\in M$ such that $M=W^*(x)$, and satisfies $G(M)=0$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}(M)=2G(M)=0$, while for every integer $n\geq3$ the free group factor $L(\mathbb F_n)$ admits finite self-adjoint generating tuples with distinct values of $\delta$ and $\delta_0$.
- **I3**: For integer word length $\ell\ge1$ and free Haar unitaries $s_1,\ldots,s_\ell$, the prefix-ratio set $S=\{p_{kj}:1\le k,j\le\ell\}$ has multiplicity $n_e=\ell$ at the identity and distinct reduced words for all off-diagonal pairs $(k,j)$, so the sum of squared multiplicities $n_p$ equals $2$ times the square of $\ell$ minus $\ell$; in the formal free group $F_S$ with generators $T_p$ for $p\in S$ and parameter $0<\alpha<1/2$, the degree-$d$ perturbation terms obey coefficientwise majorization by the nonnegative operator power and satisfy $\lVert C_d\rVert_2\le(2\alpha)^d$ via Lemma 7.
- **I4**: In the hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $R$, any countable family of commuting projections $(e_m)$ is encoded into a single self-adjoint series from which each projection $e_m$ is recovered inductively via the residual $r_m$ after subtracting $e_1,\ldots,e_{m-1}$, because the restriction of $r_m$ to $1-e_m$ lies between $0$ and $3^{-m}$ while its restriction to $e_m$ lies between $2\,3^{-m}$ and $3\,3^{-m}$, and on each tensor site the projections $d_m,q_m$ recover the matrix unit $e_{12}$ via $2d_mq_m(1-d_m)$.
- **I5**: For any unital von Neumann algebra $N=W^*(a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4)$ generated by four self-adjoint elements $a_j=a_j^*$ and any shift $C>\lVert a_1\rVert+\lVert a_2\rVert$, the two self-adjoint block matrices $A$ and $B$ in $M_2(N)$ separate the diagonal spectra into $[-\lVert a_1\rVert,\lVert a_1\rVert]$ and $[C-\lVert a_2\rVert,C+\lVert a_2\rVert]$, so spectral cutting at $\lVert a_1\rVert<\lambda<C-\lVert a_2\rVert$ recovers the matrix units $E_{ij}$ and all four corners $NE_{11}\subset Q$, giving $Q=M_2(N)$.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU1**: Inferring that an arbitrary software architecture, module graph, or Markov event-ingestion pipeline can be consolidated into two generating components or a single operator generator. -> The reduction relies on weak-operator-topology closure $W^*(S)$, spectral functional calculus, half-trace projections $\tau(p)=1/2$, and tracial ultrapowers, none of which exist in discrete software modules or classical Markov chains.
- **AU2**: Applying Theorem 1 to claim that a generic unitary perturbation generates an ambient algebra $M$ over an arbitrary subalgebra $P\subset M$ or general subsystem boundary. -> When the relative commutant $P'\cap M$ is nontrivial, Lemma 3 fails in the tracial ultrapower $\mathbf M=M^\omega$, blocking Popa's independence theorem from producing free Haar unitaries in $\mathbf Q=P^\omega$.
- **AU3**: Using Voiculescu's microstates free entropy dimensions $\delta(Z)$ or $\delta_0(Z)$ of a finite self-adjoint generating tuple $Z$ as an isomorphism invariant of the generated tracial von Neumann algebra. -> Replacing the standard semicircular $n$-tuple of $L(\mathbb F_n)$ (where $\delta$ and $\delta_0$ equal $n$) by the 2-element self-adjoint generating pair from Theorem 2 lowers both dimensions to at most $2<n$ for every integer $n\geq3$.
- **AU4**: Extrapolating fiberwise single generation of separable-predual type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors to claim that a continuous $W^*$-bundle over a compact metrizable base admits a finite family of continuous sections generating every fiber. -> The Baire category construction in Theorem 1 selects generators fiberwise without continuous dependence on parameters, and continuous $W^*$-bundles with separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ fibers can obstruct any finite generating family of continuous sections.
- **AU5**: Assuming that $G(M)=0$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}(M)=2G(M)=0$ guarantee the existence of a finite generating set and finite projection partition that attain the value $0$ in the definition of the generator invariants. -> The invariants $G$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}$ are defined as infima over finite generating sets and projection partitions; vanishing of the infimum does not produce a finite partition attaining $0$.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite constructive verification of the free-group prefix-ratio multiplicity and group-ring majorization in Proposition 8, the ternary projection recovery in Lemma 12, and the two-by-two matrix generator recovery in Lemma 14.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with keys ell, alpha, d, bits, a, C, lam
# PRECONDITIONS: ell >= 1, 0 < alpha < 1/2, d >= 1, bits in {0,1}^M, C > |a[0]| + |a[1]|, |a[0]| < lam < C - |a[1]|
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with mult_sum, distinct_S, n_e, norm_Cd_sq, norm_Ad_sq, haagerup_bound_sq, majorized, recovered_bits, recovered_a
# TIME: O(ell^(2*d) + M)
# SPACE: O(ell^(2*d) + M)
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic via fractions.Fraction
# FAILURE: raises ValueError or ZeroDivisionError and exits nonzero on invalid input or violated precondition
import sys, json, math
from fractions import Fraction

def reduce_word(w):
    st = []
    for g, sgn in w:
        if st and st[-1][0] == g and st[-1][1] == -sgn:
            st.pop()
        else:
            st.append((g, sgn))
    return tuple(st)

def mul_ring(x, y):
    out = {}
    for wx, cx in x.items():
        for wy, cy in y.items():
            w = reduce_word(wx + wy)
            v = out.get(w, Fraction(0)) + cx * cy
            if v != 0:
                out[w] = v
            elif w in out:
                del out[w]
    return out

def pow_ring(x, m):
    res = {(): Fraction(1)}
    for _ in range(m):
        res = mul_ring(res, x)
    return res

def tuples_sum(ell, d):
    if ell == 1:
        yield (d,)
        return
    for first in range(d + 1):
        for rest in tuples_sum(ell - 1, d - first):
            yield (first,) + rest

def run_case(data):
    ell = int(data["ell"])
    alpha = Fraction(data["alpha"])
    d = int(data["d"])
    if ell < 1 or not (Fraction(0) < alpha < Fraction(1, 2)) or d < 1:
        raise ValueError("bad parameters")
    counts = {}
    p_table = []
    for k in range(1, ell + 1):
        row = []
        for j in range(1, ell + 1):
            gk = [(idx, 1) for idx in range(1, k + 1)]
            gj_inv = [(idx, -1) for idx in range(j, 0, -1)]
            p = reduce_word(gk + gj_inv)
            row.append(p)
            counts[p] = counts.get(p, 0) + 1
        p_table.append(row)
    mult_sum = sum(v * v for v in counts.values())
    c = alpha / (2 * ell)
    B_list = []
    A_total = {}
    for k in range(ell):
        Bk = {}
        for j in range(ell):
            p = p_table[k][j]
            w_pos = ((p, 1),)
            w_neg = ((p, -1),)
            Bk[w_pos] = Bk.get(w_pos, Fraction(0)) + c
            Bk[w_neg] = Bk.get(w_neg, Fraction(0)) - c
            A_total[w_pos] = A_total.get(w_pos, Fraction(0)) + c
            A_total[w_neg] = A_total.get(w_neg, Fraction(0)) + c
        B_list.append(Bk)
    Cd = {}
    for tup in tuples_sum(ell, d):
        denom = Fraction(1)
        term = {(): Fraction(1)}
        for k, mk in enumerate(tup):
            denom *= math.factorial(mk)
            if mk > 0:
                term = mul_ring(term, pow_ring(B_list[k], mk))
        for w, val in term.items():
            nv = Cd.get(w, Fraction(0)) + val / denom
            if nv != 0:
                Cd[w] = nv
            elif w in Cd:
                del Cd[w]
    Ad = pow_ring(A_total, d)
    majorized = all(abs(Cd.get(w, Fraction(0))) <= Ad.get(w, Fraction(0)) for w in set(Cd) | set(Ad))
    norm_Cd_sq = sum(v * v for v in Cd.values())
    norm_Ad_sq = sum(v * v for v in Ad.values())
    haagerup_bound_sq = (2 * alpha) ** (2 * d)
    bits = [int(x) for x in data["bits"]]
    b_val = sum(Fraction(2 * bits[m - 1], 3 ** m) for m in range(1, len(bits) + 1))
    rec_bits = []
    for m in range(1, len(bits) + 1):
        rm = b_val - sum(Fraction(2 * rec_bits[j - 1], 3 ** j) for j in range(1, m))
        thresh = Fraction(3, 2) * Fraction(1, 3 ** m)
        rec_bits.append(1 if rm > thresh else 0)
    a = [Fraction(x) for x in data["a"]]
    C_shift = Fraction(data["C"])
    lam = Fraction(data["lam"])
    if not (abs(a[0]) < lam < C_shift - abs(a[1])):
        raise ValueError("spectral separation violated")
    a11_diag = a[0]
    a22_diag = a[1] + C_shift
    e11_1 = 1 if a11_diag < lam else 0
    e11_2 = 1 if a22_diag < lam else 0
    rec_a1 = e11_1 * a11_diag
    rec_a2 = (1 - e11_2) * a22_diag - C_shift
    rec_a3 = e11_1 * a[2]
    rec_a4 = (1 - e11_2) * a[3]
    return {
        "mult_sum": mult_sum,
        "distinct_S": len(counts),
        "n_e": counts.get((), 0),
        "norm_Cd_sq": str(norm_Cd_sq),
        "norm_Ad_sq": str(norm_Ad_sq),
        "haagerup_bound_sq": str(haagerup_bound_sq),
        "majorized": majorized,
        "recovered_bits": rec_bits,
        "recovered_a": [str(rec_a1), str(rec_a2), str(rec_a3), str(rec_a4)]
    }

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print(json.dumps(run_case(json.loads(sys.stdin.read())), sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys, json\nfrom flint import fmpz, fmpq, fmpq_mat\n\nraw_in = open(sys.argv[1], encoding=\"utf-8\").read()\nraw_out = open(sys.argv[2], encoding=\"utf-8\").read().strip()\nexp_str = sys.argv[3].strip()\nif not raw_out:\n    sys.exit(2)\ntry:\n    inp = json.loads(raw_in)\n    got = json.loads(raw_out)\n    exp = json.loads(exp_str)\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(3)\n\nell = int(inp[\"ell\"])\nalpha = fmpq(inp[\"alpha\"])\nd = int(inp[\"d\"])\n\n# Independent formula check for Proposition 8 multiplicity identity\nexpected_mult_sum = 2 * ell * ell - ell\nexpected_distinct = ell * (ell - 1) + 1\nif got.get(\"mult_sum\") != expected_mult_sum or got.get(\"distinct_S\") != expected_distinct or got.get(\"n_e\") != ell:\n    sys.exit(4)\n\n# Independent group-ring computation in F_S using integer generator codes\n# Map identity ratio to generator 0, and each off-diagonal pair (k, j) to a unique positive generator id\ngen_map = {}\nnext_id = 1\nfor k in range(ell):\n    for j in range(ell):\n        if k == j:\n            gen_map[(k, j)] = 0\n        else:\n            gen_map[(k, j)] = next_id\n            next_id += 1\n\ndef red(seq):\n    out = []\n    for g in seq:\n        if out and out[-1] == -g:\n            out.pop()\n        else:\n            out.append(g)\n    return tuple(out)\n\ndef combine(p, q):\n    acc = {}\n    for wp, vp in p.items():\n        for wq, vq in q.items():\n            w = red(wp + wq)\n            acc[w] = acc.get(w, fmpq(0)) + vp * vq\n    return {k: v for k, v in acc.items() if v != 0}\n\nc_val = alpha / fmpq(2 * ell)\nrow_B = []\nA_op = {}\nfor k in range(ell):\n    bk = {}\n    for j in range(ell):\n        gid = gen_map[(k, j)] + 1\n        bk[(gid,)] = bk.get((gid,), fmpq(0)) + c_val\n        bk[(-gid,)] = bk.get((-gid,), fmpq(0)) - c_val\n        A_op[(gid,)] = A_op.get((gid,), fmpq(0)) + c_val\n        A_op[(-gid,)] = A_op.get((-gid,), fmpq(0)) + c_val\n    row_B.append(bk)\n\n# Compute degree-d homogeneous part of prod_{k=1}^ell exp(B_k) via dynamic programming over k\ndp = {0: {(): fmpq(1)}}\nfor k in range(ell):\n    ndp = {deg: {} for deg in range(d + 1)}\n    powers = [{(): fmpq(1)}]\n    fact = fmpz(1)\n    for m in range(1, d + 1):\n        fact *= m\n        pw = combine(powers[-1], row_B[k])\n        powers.append({w: v for w, v in pw.items()})\n    for deg_prev, poly_prev in dp.items():\n        f_m = fmpz(1)\n        for m in range(d - deg_prev + 1):\n            if m > 0:\n                f_m *= m\n            scaled = {w: v / fmpq(f_m) for w, v in powers[m].items()}\n            prod_poly = combine(poly_prev, scaled)\n            target = ndp[deg_prev + m]\n            for w, v in prod_poly.items():\n                target[w] = target.get(w, fmpq(0)) + v\n    dp = {deg: {w: v for w, v in poly.items() if v != 0} for deg, poly in ndp.items()}\n\nC_deg = dp[d]\nA_deg = {(): fmpq(1)}\nfor _ in range(d):\n    A_deg = combine(A_deg, A_op)\n\nfor w in set(C_deg) | set(A_deg):\n    cv = C_deg.get(w, fmpq(0))\n    av = A_deg.get(w, fmpq(0))\n    if (cv if cv >= 0 else -cv) > av:\n        sys.exit(5)\n\nif not got.get(\"majorized\"):\n    sys.exit(6)\n\nnorm_c_sq = sum((v * v for v in C_deg.values()), fmpq(0))\nnorm_a_sq = sum((v * v for v in A_deg.values()), fmpq(0))\nhaag_sq = (fmpq(2) * alpha) ** (2 * d)\n\nif not (norm_c_sq <= norm_a_sq <= haag_sq):\n    sys.exit(7)\nif got.get(\"norm_Cd_sq\") != str(norm_c_sq) or got.get(\"norm_Ad_sq\") != str(norm_a_sq) or got.get(\"haagerup_bound_sq\") != str(haag_sq):\n    sys.exit(8)\n\n# Independent check of Lemma 12 projection recovery\nbits_in = [int(x) for x in inp[\"bits\"]]\nif got.get(\"recovered_bits\") != bits_in:\n    sys.exit(9)\n\n# Independent check of Lemma 14 2x2 matrix algebra recovery using fmpq_mat\na_in = [fmpq(x) for x in inp[\"a\"]]\nC_in = fmpq(inp[\"C\"])\nA_mat = fmpq_mat([[a_in[0], 0], [0, a_in[1] + C_in]])\nB_mat = fmpq_mat([[a_in[2], 1], [1, a_in[3]]])\nE11 = fmpq_mat([[1, 0], [0, 0]])\nE22 = fmpq_mat([[0, 0], [0, 1]])\nE12 = E11 * B_mat * E22\nE21 = E12.transpose()\nrec_mat_a = [\n    str((E11 * A_mat * E11)[0, 0]),\n    str((E12 * A_mat * E21 - C_in * E11)[0, 0]),\n    str((E11 * B_mat * E11)[0, 0]),\n    str((E12 * B_mat * E21)[0, 0])\n]\nif got.get(\"recovered_a\") != rec_mat_a or got.get(\"recovered_a\") != [str(x) for x in a_in]:\n    sys.exit(10)\n\n# Check against expected vector payload\nfor k, v in exp.items():\n    if got.get(k) != v:\n        sys.exit(11)\n\nsys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "Single-line JSON object on stdout reporting the prefix-ratio multiplicity sum, distinct ratio count, identity multiplicity, exact rational squared 2-norms of the degree-d expansion and majorant, Haagerup bound, majorization boolean, recovered projection bits from Lemma 12, and recovered four self-adjoint generators from Lemma 14.",
    "reason": "Finite constructive verification of the free-group prefix-ratio multiplicity and group-ring majorization in Proposition 8, the ternary projection recovery in Lemma 12, and the two-by-two matrix generator recovery in Lemma 14.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object on stdin with keys ell, alpha, d, bits, a, C, lam\n# PRECONDITIONS: ell >= 1, 0 < alpha < 1/2, d >= 1, bits in {0,1}^M, C > |a[0]| + |a[1]|, |a[0]| < lam < C - |a[1]|\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object with mult_sum, distinct_S, n_e, norm_Cd_sq, norm_Ad_sq, haagerup_bound_sq, majorized, recovered_bits, recovered_a\n# TIME: O(ell^(2*d) + M)\n# SPACE: O(ell^(2*d) + M)\n# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic via fractions.Fraction\n# FAILURE: raises ValueError or ZeroDivisionError and exits nonzero on invalid input or violated precondition\nimport sys, json, math\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef reduce_word(w):\n    st = []\n    for g, sgn in w:\n        if st and st[-1][0] == g and st[-1][1] == -sgn:\n            st.pop()\n        else:\n            st.append((g, sgn))\n    return tuple(st)\n\ndef mul_ring(x, y):\n    out = {}\n    for wx, cx in x.items():\n        for wy, cy in y.items():\n            w = reduce_word(wx + wy)\n            v = out.get(w, Fraction(0)) + cx * cy\n            if v != 0:\n                out[w] = v\n            elif w in out:\n                del out[w]\n    return out\n\ndef pow_ring(x, m):\n    res = {(): Fraction(1)}\n    for _ in range(m):\n        res = mul_ring(res, x)\n    return res\n\ndef tuples_sum(ell, d):\n    if ell == 1:\n        yield (d,)\n        return\n    for first in range(d + 1):\n        for rest in tuples_sum(ell - 1, d - first):\n            yield (first,) + rest\n\ndef run_case(data):\n    ell = int(data[\"ell\"])\n    alpha = Fraction(data[\"alpha\"])\n    d = int(data[\"d\"])\n    if ell < 1 or not (Fraction(0) < alpha < Fraction(1, 2)) or d < 1:\n        raise ValueError(\"bad parameters\")\n    counts = {}\n    p_table = []\n    for k in range(1, ell + 1):\n        row = []\n        for j in range(1, ell + 1):\n            gk = [(idx, 1) for idx in range(1, k + 1)]\n            gj_inv = [(idx, -1) for idx in range(j, 0, -1)]\n            p = reduce_word(gk + gj_inv)\n            row.append(p)\n            counts[p] = counts.get(p, 0) + 1\n        p_table.append(row)\n    mult_sum = sum(v * v for v in counts.values())\n    c = alpha / (2 * ell)\n    B_list = []\n    A_total = {}\n    for k in range(ell):\n        Bk = {}\n        for j in range(ell):\n            p = p_table[k][j]\n            w_pos = ((p, 1),)\n            w_neg = ((p, -1),)\n            Bk[w_pos] = Bk.get(w_pos, Fraction(0)) + c\n            Bk[w_neg] = Bk.get(w_neg, Fraction(0)) - c\n            A_total[w_pos] = A_total.get(w_pos, Fraction(0)) + c\n            A_total[w_neg] = A_total.get(w_neg, Fraction(0)) + c\n        B_list.append(Bk)\n    Cd = {}\n    for tup in tuples_sum(ell, d):\n        denom = Fraction(1)\n        term = {(): Fraction(1)}\n        for k, mk in enumerate(tup):\n            denom *= math.factorial(mk)\n            if mk > 0:\n                term = mul_ring(term, pow_ring(B_list[k], mk))\n        for w, val in term.items():\n            nv = Cd.get(w, Fraction(0)) + val / denom\n            if nv != 0:\n                Cd[w] = nv\n            elif w in Cd:\n                del Cd[w]\n    Ad = pow_ring(A_total, d)\n    majorized = all(abs(Cd.get(w, Fraction(0))) <= Ad.get(w, Fraction(0)) for w in set(Cd) | set(Ad))\n    norm_Cd_sq = sum(v * v for v in Cd.values())\n    norm_Ad_sq = sum(v * v for v in Ad.values())\n    haagerup_bound_sq = (2 * alpha) ** (2 * d)\n    bits = [int(x) for x in data[\"bits\"]]\n    b_val = sum(Fraction(2 * bits[m - 1], 3 ** m) for m in range(1, len(bits) + 1))\n    rec_bits = []\n    for m in range(1, len(bits) + 1):\n        rm = b_val - sum(Fraction(2 * rec_bits[j - 1], 3 ** j) for j in range(1, m))\n        thresh = Fraction(3, 2) * Fraction(1, 3 ** m)\n        rec_bits.append(1 if rm > thresh else 0)\n    a = [Fraction(x) for x in data[\"a\"]]\n    C_shift = Fraction(data[\"C\"])\n    lam = Fraction(data[\"lam\"])\n    if not (abs(a[0]) < lam < C_shift - abs(a[1])):\n        raise ValueError(\"spectral separation violated\")\n    a11_diag = a[0]\n    a22_diag = a[1] + C_shift\n    e11_1 = 1 if a11_diag < lam else 0\n    e11_2 = 1 if a22_diag < lam else 0\n    rec_a1 = e11_1 * a11_diag\n    rec_a2 = (1 - e11_2) * a22_diag - C_shift\n    rec_a3 = e11_1 * a[2]\n    rec_a4 = (1 - e11_2) * a[3]\n    return {\n        \"mult_sum\": mult_sum,\n        \"distinct_S\": len(counts),\n        \"n_e\": counts.get((), 0),\n        \"norm_Cd_sq\": str(norm_Cd_sq),\n        \"norm_Ad_sq\": str(norm_Ad_sq),\n        \"haagerup_bound_sq\": str(haagerup_bound_sq),\n        \"majorized\": majorized,\n        \"recovered_bits\": rec_bits,\n        \"recovered_a\": [str(rec_a1), str(rec_a2), str(rec_a3), str(rec_a4)]\n    }\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    print(json.dumps(run_case(json.loads(sys.stdin.read())), sort_keys=True))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "{\"distinct_S\": 1, \"haagerup_bound_sq\": \"1/4\", \"majorized\": true, \"mult_sum\": 1, \"n_e\": 1, \"norm_Ad_sq\": \"1/32\", \"norm_Cd_sq\": \"1/32\", \"recovered_a\": [\"1/2\", \"-1/3\", \"2/5\", \"0\"], \"recovered_bits\": [1]}",
        "input": "{\"ell\": 1, \"alpha\": \"1/4\", \"d\": 1, \"bits\": [1], \"a\": [\"1/2\", \"-1/3\", \"2/5\", \"0\"], \"C\": \"2\", \"lam\": \"1\"}"
      },

        "input": "{\"ell\": 1, \"alpha\": \"1/8\", \"d\": 2, \"bits\": [0, 1, 0], \"a\": [\"0\", \"0\", \"-1\", \"1\"], \"C\": \"1\", \"lam\": \"1/2\"}"
      },

        "input": "{\"ell\": 2, \"alpha\": \"1/4\", \"d\": 1, \"bits\": [1, 0, 1, 1], \"a\": [\"-3/4\", \"1/2\", \"7/3\", \"-5/2\"], \"C\": \"3\", \"lam\": \"1\"}"

        "input": "{\"ell\": 2, \"alpha\": \"1/5\", \"d\": 2, \"bits\": [0, 0, 1, 0, 1], \"a\": [\"2/3\", \"-3/5\", \"1/7\", \"4/9\"], \"C\": \"3\", \"lam\": \"1\"}"

        "input": "{\"ell\": 3, \"alpha\": \"1/6\", \"d\": 1, \"bits\": [1, 1, 0, 1], \"a\": [\"1/3\", \"1/4\", \"-2/3\", \"5/6\"], \"C\": \"2\", \"lam\": \"1\"}"
      },

        "input": "{\"ell\": 3, \"alpha\": \"1/4\", \"d\": 2, \"bits\": [0, 1, 1, 0, 1], \"a\": [\"-1/5\", \"2/7\", \"3/8\", \"-9/10\"], \"C\": \"2\", \"lam\": \"1\"}"

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a software system architecture, module interface, or service consolidation contract. The paper establishes single operator generation and two self-adjoint generation for abstract type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable predual in the weak operator topology. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a game mechanic, interactive simulation model, or finite dynamical simulator. The perturbation construction takes place in a tracial ultrapower $\mathbf M=M^\omega$ and selects a coordinate index $n$ nonconstructively along a free ultrafilter $\omega$. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an executable software tool, solver interface, or automated verification pipeline. Conditional expectations $E_N$, Borel functional calculus, and Baire category arguments are used purely as mathematical proof mechanisms. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a terminating algorithm or complexity bound. Although Lemma 12 and Lemma 14 give explicit spectral formulas for recovering commuting projections and $2\times2$ matrix units via Borel functional calculus, the overall generation theorems rely on nonconstructive ultrafilter limits, subfactor embeddings, and Baire category. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a computer data structure, indexing layout, or memory hierarchy contract. The tensor product and $2\times2$ matrix algebras are operator-algebraic constructions closed in the weak operator topology. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a hardware accelerator architecture, circuit design, or finite-precision numeric execution model. The proofs operate on infinite-dimensional Hilbert spaces and abstract von Neumann algebras. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a cryptographic primitive, threat model, hardness assumption, or security reduction. Free Haar unitaries and free entropy dimensions are tracial operator-algebraic concepts. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a statistical learning model, training objective, dataset assumption, or generalization bound. The paper addresses operator-algebraic generation in type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: an operations-research optimization problem, decision variable model, or mathematical programming solver guarantee. Infima and Hilbert-space convex hull projections appear only as analytic proof steps. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: a distributed computing model, message-passing protocol, consistency guarantee, or fault-tolerance mechanism. The paper studies inclusions of tracial von Neumann algebras. |
| other (operator_algebras) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Directly solves the generator problem for type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable predual (Theorem 2) and establishes generic relative unitary generation for irreducible inclusions $P\subset M$ (Theorem 1). Combined with Willig's direct-integral reduction, it implies single generation of every von Neumann algebra with separable predual and forces the generator invariants $G(M)=0$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}(M)=2G(M)=0$. An executable software implementation is ABSENT. |
| other (free_probability) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The proof constructs successive free Haar unitaries in tracial ultrapowers $\mathbf Q=P^\omega\subset M^\omega=\mathbf M$ via Popa's independence theorem, establishes freeness preservation under absorption of base-algebra unitaries, and proves that Voiculescu's microstates free entropy dimensions $\delta$ and $\delta_0$ depend on the choice of finite self-adjoint generating tuple for $L(\mathbb F_n)$ when $n\geq3$. An executable software implementation is ABSENT. |
| other (functional_analysis) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper develops operator-norm and trace $2$-norm estimates in Hilbert space, including the length-one Haagerup inequality on $\ell^2(F)$, uniform exponential remainder majorization, metric completeness of $\mathcal U(M)$, Baire category for lower semicontinuous $L^2$ projection norms, and Borel spectral projection recovery in $P=W^*(a,b)$ and $M_2(N)$. A finite computational realization is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **LIM-01** (hypothesis): Single generation and two-element self-adjoint generation require the von Neumann algebra to have a separable predual, which ensures separability of $L^2(M,\tau)$ for the Baire category intersection over a countable dense subset without requiring operator-norm separability of $M$. Blocked use: ['Applying Theorem 2 or Theorem 1 to von Neumann algebras or type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors whose predual is not separable', 'Equating separability of the predual with operator-norm separability of $M$']
- **LIM-02** (hypothesis): Relative generation by a single unitary in Theorem 1 requires an irreducible inclusion $P\subset M$ of type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors satisfying $P'\cap M=\mathbb C1$, which guarantees that the ultrapower inclusion $\mathbf Q\subset\mathbf M$ has trivial relative commutant $\mathbf Q'\cap\mathbf M$ in Lemma 3. Blocked use: ["Applying relative unitary generation to reducible subfactor inclusions where $P'\\cap M=\\mathbb C1$ does not hold", 'Using Theorem 1 to consolidate arbitrary subsystem or modular boundaries lacking a tracial factor structure and trivial relative commutant']
- **LIM-03** (counterexample_scope): Pointwise single generation of each separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ fiber does not extend to continuous families: there exists a $W^*$-bundle over a compact metrizable base with separable-predual $\mathrm{II}_1$ fibers for which no finite family of continuous sections generates every fiber. Blocked use: ['Inferring existence of continuous generating sections across a parameterized family or $W^*$-bundle of type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factors from fiberwise single generation']
- **LIM-04** (nonconstructive): The existence proof for the relative generating unitary and the general single generator is nonconstructive: it relies on a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, nonconstructive coordinate selection in the tracial ultrapower $\mathbf M$, Baire category on the complete metric space $(\mathcal U(M),d)$ to locate continuity points, and Popa's irreducible hyperfinite embedding theorem. Blocked use: ['Treating Theorem 1 or Theorem 2 as an explicit numerical algorithm that outputs a generating operator $x$ for a general non-hyperfinite $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor without oracle access to ultrafilter limits and Baire category witnesses']
- **LIM-05** (approximation): The uniform remainder estimate in Proposition 8 requires $0<\alpha<1/2$ so that the geometric series bound converges, while the proof of Theorem 1 restricts the parameter $\alpha$ further below 1/4 and one over 32 times the norm of the orthogonal residual $\xi\in M$ and selects unitaries depending separately on each integer word length $\ell\ge1$. Blocked use: ['Applying the geometric remainder bound when $0<\\alpha<1/2$ is violated', 'Assuming the perturbed unitaries $\\widetilde u\\in\\mathcal U(M)$ and $\\widetilde t_0,\\ldots,\\widetilde t_\\ell\\in\\mathcal U(P)$ in Proposition 8 are chosen independently of the word length $\\ell$']
- **LIM-06** (edge_case): Although the generator invariants $G$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}$ satisfy $G(M)=0$ and $G_{\mathrm{sa}}(M)=2G(M)=0$ for every complex $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $M$ with separable predual and normalized trace, these zero values are only the defining infima over finite generating sets and finite projection partitions; no generating set or projection partition attaining zero is asserted. Blocked use: ['Assuming the existence of an extremal finite generating set or projection partition that attains zero in the definition of $G(M)=0$ or $G_{\\mathrm{sa}}(M)=2G(M)=0$']
- **LIM-07** (counterexample_scope): For every integer $n\geq3$, Voiculescu's microstates free entropy dimension $\delta$ and modified dimension $\delta_0$ take different values on two finite self-adjoint generating tuples of the free group factor $L(\mathbb F_n)$ (namely $n$ on a semicircular $n$-tuple and at most $2<n$ on a two-element generating pair), so neither $\delta$ nor $\delta_0$ is an invariant of the generated tracial von Neumann algebra. Blocked use: ['Treating microstates free entropy dimension $\\delta(Z)$ or $\\delta_0(Z)$ of a finite self-adjoint generating tuple $Z$ as an invariant of the generated tracial von Neumann algebra']
- **LIM-08** (formal_scope): The internal proofs apply to type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors (including division of the unit into a projection $p\in M$ with $\tau(p)=1/2$ to identify $M$ with $M_2(N)$ over the half-trace corner $N=pMp$); extension to general von Neumann algebras with separable predual relies on external results for type I, properly infinite, and Willig's direct-integral reduction, and the stronger fixed-factor statement on $L(\mathbb F_2)$ cites an external free group factor isomorphism preprint. Blocked use: ['Applying the half-trace corner decomposition over $N=pMp$ directly to finite-dimensional algebras of odd dimension or non-diffuse algebras lacking a projection $p\\in M$ with $\\tau(p)=1/2$', "Citing this paper alone for the proof of Willig's direct-integral reduction or the free group factor isomorphism on $L(\\mathbb F_2)$"]

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 296 (`lean/docs/296.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Relative generation and the generator problem for finite factors](../../preprints/Relative-generation-and-the-generator-problem-for-finite-factors-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization answers the generator problem affirmatively for type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable predual: one bounded operator generates the factor under adjoints and weak-operator closure. It imposes no separability assumption on the representing Hilbert space or on the operator-norm topology.
>
> It also proves the paper's relative-generation result. For an irreducible inclusion of type $\mathrm{II}_1$ factors with separable predual for the larger factor, the unitaries that generate the larger factor together with the smaller one form a dense $G_\delta$ set in the normalized trace two-norm topology. The normalized faithful normal trace is included in the assertion.

Comparator source `RelativeGeneration.lean` (5992 bytes, sha256 `d8777c8421d1b134d75467b82277d46d4e0ae80cc6d3f8d4909a54abd4da6d70`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe level

namespace RelativeGeneration

variable {Hilbert : Type level} [NormedAddCommGroup Hilbert]
  [InnerProductSpace ℂ Hilbert] [CompleteSpace Hilbert]

/-- Weak operator closedness of a concrete unital star subalgebra. -/
def WOTClosed (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) : Prop :=
  IsClosed {operator : Hilbert →WOT[ℂ] Hilbert | operator.toCLM ∈ algebra}

/-- The weakly closed unital star algebra generated by a set of operators. -/
def wstar (operators : Set (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) :
    StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert) :=
  sInf {algebra | WOTClosed algebra ∧ operators ⊆ algebra}

/-- A nonzero, weakly closed, finite diffuse algebra with scalar center. -/
structure IsII1Factor (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) : Prop where
  nonzero : (0 : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert) ≠ 1
  weaklyClosed : WOTClosed algebra
  factor : ∀ element : algebra, (∀ other : algebra, other * element = element * other) →
    ∃ scalar : ℂ, element = algebraMap ℂ algebra scalar
  finite : ∀ isometry : algebra, star isometry * isometry = 1 → isometry * star isometry = 1
  diffuse : ∀ projection : algebra, IsStarProjection projection → projection ≠ 0 →
    ∃ subprojection : algebra, IsStarProjection subprojection ∧ subprojection ≠ 0 ∧
      subprojection ≠ projection ∧ projection * subprojection = subprojection ∧
      subprojection * projection = subprojection

/-- A same-universe separable Banach predual, with the conjugate-linear dual convention.
This imposes no separability on the Hilbert representation or the operator norm topology. -/
def HasSeparablePredual (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) : Prop :=
  ∃ (Banach : Type level) (_ : NormedAddCommGroup Banach) (_ : NormedSpace ℂ Banach)
    (_ : CompleteSpace Banach) (_ : TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace Banach),
    Nonempty (StrongDual ℂ Banach ≃ₗᵢ⋆[ℂ] algebra)

/-- The ultraweak topology is generated by absolutely summable vector functionals. -/
abbrev ultraweakTopology (Hilbert : Type level) [NormedAddCommGroup Hilbert]
    [InnerProductSpace ℂ Hilbert] [CompleteSpace Hilbert] :
    TopologicalSpace (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert) :=
  ⨅ (left : ℕ → Hilbert) (right : ℕ → Hilbert)
    (_ : Summable (fun index => ‖left index‖ * ‖right index‖)),
    TopologicalSpace.induced
      (fun operator : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert =>
        ∑' index, inner ℂ (left index) (operator (right index))) inferInstance

/-- The concrete algebra carries the induced ultraweak topology. -/
abbrev algebraUltraweakTopology (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) :
    TopologicalSpace algebra :=
  TopologicalSpace.induced (fun operator : algebra => (operator : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert))
    (ultraweakTopology Hilbert)

/-- A faithful normal normalized trace, not an additional hypothesis on the inclusion. -/
structure IsNormalizedTrace (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert))
    (trace : algebra →L[ℂ] ℂ) : Prop where
  normalized : trace 1 = 1
  positive : ∀ element : algebra, 0 ≤ (trace (star element * element)).re
  star_preserving : ∀ element : algebra, trace (star element) = star (trace element)
  tracial : ∀ left right : algebra, trace (left * right) = trace (right * left)
  faithful : ∀ element : algebra, trace (star element * element) = 0 → element = 0
  normal : @Continuous algebra ℂ (algebraUltraweakTopology algebra) inferInstance trace

/-- The normalized trace two-distance between genuine unitaries of the original algebra. -/
def traceDistance (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert))
    (trace : algebra →L[ℂ] ℂ) (left right : unitary algebra) : ℝ :=
  Real.sqrt (trace (star ((left : algebra) - (right : algebra)) *
    ((left : algebra) - (right : algebra)))).re

/-- The topology generated by trace two-distance balls, not the inherited operator norm topology. -/
abbrev traceTopology (algebra : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert))
    (trace : algebra →L[ℂ] ℂ) : TopologicalSpace (unitary algebra) :=
  TopologicalSpace.generateFrom {ball | ∃ center : unitary algebra, ∃ radius : ℝ,
    0 < radius ∧ ball = {point | traceDistance algebra trace center point < radius}}

/-- The entire relative-generator locus in the original unitary group. -/
def relativeGeneratorLocus (small large : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) :
    Set (unitary large) :=
  {element | wstar ((small : Set (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)) ∪
    {((element : large) : Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)}) = large}

/-- Every irreducible inclusion of concrete type II₁ factors with separable predual
of the larger factor has a dense Gδ full relative-generator locus in the normalized
trace two-topology. The normalized faithful normal trace is asserted to exist;
no representation, transport, cyclicity, or perturbation premise is imposed. -/
def MainTarget : Prop :=
  ∀ (Hilbert : Type level) [NormedAddCommGroup Hilbert] [InnerProductSpace ℂ Hilbert]
    [CompleteSpace Hilbert] (small large : StarSubalgebra ℂ (Hilbert →L[ℂ] Hilbert)),
    IsII1Factor small → IsII1Factor large → ∀ inclusion : small ≤ large,
      (∀ element : large,
        (∀ member : small, StarSubalgebra.inclusion inclusion member * element =
          element * StarSubalgebra.inclusion inclusion member) →
        ∃ scalar : ℂ, element = algebraMap ℂ large scalar) →
      HasSeparablePredual large →
      ∃ trace : large →L[ℂ] ℂ, IsNormalizedTrace large trace ∧
        @IsGδ (unitary large) (traceTopology large trace) (relativeGeneratorLocus small large) ∧
        @Dense (unitary large) (traceTopology large trace) (relativeGeneratorLocus small large)

theorem main_theorem : MainTarget.{level} := by
  sorry

end RelativeGeneration

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every type II<sub>1</sub> factor with separable predual is generated by one operator, equivalently by two self-adjoint operators. Combined with the established direct-integral reduction to this case, this gives an affirmative solution of the generator problem for von Neumann algebras with separable predual.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
